# Products of Tomorrow — Personal agents research package

**The evidence supports a real movement toward intent-led delegation. It does not yet establish that personal agents will become the dominant way people operate digital products.** The strongest defensible future claim is conditional: agents can become the preferred operator where permitted access, verified results and recovery reduce total human work enough that people choose delegation again. App infrastructure, visual choice and specialist servicing can remain underneath an agent entry point. This is the planner's synthesis judgment, supported by the complete final synthesis and its Retain material below.

Round2 strengthens the early signs: actual action-use datasets, a named conversational flight-booking route, and documented transaction reconciliation. It also sharpens the limits: none of the inspected behavioral candidates measures displaced app journeys plus all-in human effort; the flight route retains partner servicing boundaries; a verified unintended external action occurred during internal evaluation. A complete family holiday remains an analytical illustration, not a demonstrated end-to-end agent success.

Read [final synthesis](#stage-9) for the argument, tensions and falsifier; [round2 findings](#stage-8) for what changed; [final source/evidence reconciliation](#stage-10) for auditability. Complete background, role perspectives, grounded questions, both syntheses and every targeted finding are embedded below. Original worker paths are provenance only; this document stands alone.

## Frame

Topic verbatim: **Personal agents will become dominant over the app way of working. Personal agents shift interaction from operating interfaces to expressing intent and receiving outcomes.**

Book: Products of Tomorrow, nonfiction about future digital products for a broad audience, including personal and professional work. The investigation asks where the world is moving, why, what early signs support the hypothesis, competing futures and conditions/limits. GenAI lens: intent interpretation, dynamic planning, tools, persistent context and delegated action. A personal agent crosses product boundaries; an intent-based feature or bounded specialist agent is not automatically equivalent.

Original research as-of date: **2026-10-10**. Continuation/retrieval date: **2026-10-11**. Historical studies keep their actual observation windows; undated live documents cannot certify their precise previous-day state. No supplied book-state file, fabricated interview data, live purchase, external publication or chapter draft. The family-holiday journey covers preferences, dates, budget, bookings and changes as an illustrative analytical case. Full autonomous research was authorized. One planner owns the canonical document; workers used fresh contexts and separate artifacts.

Canonical destination: `/workspace/scratch/d3a925ca4c52/personal-agents-research.md`. Complete frame and frozen decisions appear in [Step1](#stage-1).

## Question Map

Stable initial Q001–008 and grounded children Q009–022 remain question-led. The full [expanded map/history](#stage-4) preserves each question's purpose, parent, owner, evidence need and dependencies; the [final status/map sidecar](#stage-9) supplies final links and answers. All22 remain tracked: **18 partial, 4 open, 0 answered**. Open: Q010 (sustained app substitution), Q014 (equivalent-capability interface form), Q015 (repeated traveler net benefit), Q021 (repeat shopping displacement). The count does not imply no useful knowledge: broad questions remain partial despite well-supported narrow answers.

## Expert Panel & Assignments

Seven simulated human company-role archetypes cover PM, UX research, AI/software engineering, risk/governance, business leadership/economics, marketing and sales. They are analytical lenses, not interviews or employee testimony. Platform orchestration, marketplace control, merchant distribution, measured user benefit, technical capability, governance and sales incentives are intentionally in tension. The [complete panel](#stage-3) records company context, rationale and blind spots; role-specific questions are preserved in [Step4](#stage-4).

UX research follows **current pain → proposed agent solution → shape/form → validation** (Q012→Q013→Q014→Q015). Observed comparison work supports bounded assistance candidates; household roles, preferred intervention, comparative interface winner and repeated full-lifecycle net benefit remain unresolved. A visual itinerary with inspectable actions is a testable proposal, not a proven best form.

## Knowledge Map

Question dependencies are preserved in the initial/expanded maps. Synthesis1's [concept registry and relationship map](#stage-6) and synthesis2's [updated registry/map](#stage-9) identify sourced mechanisms separately from author inference. New relationships concern observed oversight, legitimate external-action boundaries, partner servicing and state reconciliation. None upgrades a benchmark, policy or rollout into demonstrated dominance.

Original tally: 62 cards, Retain36/Downgrade26, six unsupported extensions removed rather than cards erased. Its conclusion and uncertainty remain historically intact.

## Synthesis 2 Handoff

[Final fresh synthesis2](#stage-9) rebuilds the argument from combined evidence, accounts all74 cards, preserves live tensions and disconfirming evidence, updates all22 question statuses and G outcomes, and records changed versus unchanged claims. No additional synthesis search was needed because actual prior question-linked tests covered the weakening conditions. Framing remains a conditional future hypothesis; the alternative of pervasive agent assistance with retained specialist surfaces remains viable.

## Final Research Package & Completeness

All10 workflow steps and complete stage outputs are included. Background achieved rungs1–10: **4,4,5,4,5,5,4,2,3,2 =38**, twelve short of the original five-per-rung goal. Rung5 includes five major-platform findings; rung6 includes five named cases. All ten rungs were attempted in background and every focused research brief. Targeted cards add depth and do not erase historical background shortfalls or count shared sources twice. Missing newsletter/current-outcome breadth remains disclosed.

The decisive remaining test is sustained voluntary substitution: do the same eligible people complete recurring outcomes with less total specification, approval, checking, correction and recovery work, and actually reduce direct app operation after failures and dropouts are included? A prospective matched study across personal and professional categories could strengthen or falsify the mechanism. The precise observable falsifier and final twelve-field synthesis completeness are in [Step9](#stage-9); package verification is in [Step10](#stage-10).

# Products of Tomorrow — Personal agents research frame

As of: 2026-10-10. Topic verbatim: Personal agents will become dominant over the app way of working. Personal agents shift interaction from operating interfaces to expressing intent and receiving outcomes.

Objective: understand future direction, mechanisms, early signs, competing futures, conditions and limits. Dominance is a working hypothesis, not an established finding. Broad nonfiction audience. GenAI lens: natural-language intent interpretation, planning, tool use, memory and delegated action; distinguish intent-based apps from a persistent cross-app personal agent. Include personal and professional products. Horizon: evidence available as of the research date, with explicit near-term versus speculative longer-term forecasts. No book state supplied beyond this brief.

Illustrative journey: arrange a family holiday with preferences, dates, budget, bookings and changes. This is an analytical scenario, not observed user pain or proof of complete shipped capability. Exclude chapter drafting, external posting and fabricated interview data. Full autonomous workflow authorized. Final destination: /workspace/scratch/d3a925ca4c52/personal-agents-research.md. Planner alone writes it; workers write separate run-personal-agents artifacts.

# Personal agents — Background Research — 2026-10-10

## Research Objective

Topic verbatim: Personal agents will become dominant over the app way of working. Personal agents shift interaction from operating interfaces to expressing intent and receiving outcomes.

Investigate future direction, enabling mechanisms, early signs, competing futures and conditions for this working hypothesis. Products of Tomorrow is broad nonfiction, including personal and professional products. GenAI enables interpretation, planning, memory and tool execution; distinguish a persistent cross-app agent from chat, a fixed automation or an intent-based feature inside an app. Evidence is current through 2026-10-10 where verified; older experiments are explicitly historical. No supplied book-state document, interviews or chapter drafting. The family-holiday journey is an analytical scenario, not observed user pain or demonstrated complete automation.

Ground-floor check: five already offered products are OpenAI dots (restricted paid-plan rollout), Meta Muse (US rollout), Claude Cowork/Code computer use (research preview), Alexa+ (all-US availability), and Cursor Bugbot (released bounded review agent). Gemini’s Oct8 universal work agent remains an announcement in this brief because precise rollout access was not established. ChatGPT agent’s July2025 launch page was freshly opened and explicitly calls itself outdated: https://openai.com/index/introducing-chatgpt-agent/ (turn6view3); its broad travel marketing is not current verified completion evidence.

Senior-PM blind spot asked: “What would an experienced PM miss by reading capability launches alone?” Method: compare availability text, repeated-task reliability, latency, privacy revocation, platform access and named customer outcomes. Findings added at their proper rungs: E-BG-009–017 mechanisms/reliability, E-BG-031 granular-memory limits, E-BG-038 platform refusal. Main missing evidence is ordinary users’ sustained delegation behavior and net time saved after oversight. This is an inference from the assembled evidence, not a user interview finding.

Question answers (all partial; capability scope is answered for selected products, not the whole market):

- Q001: Persistent agents have entered restricted and consumer rollout, alongside previews and pilots. Dots/Muse and Claude/Alexa are concrete starting points; Google’s latest announced scope has unverified access. Counterevidence: Mariner shutdown, preview limitations. Confidence high on quoted availability, medium on breadth, low on success frequency. E-BG-018–022,037; exact sources below.
- Q002: Support-volume, multilingual service and artifact-review work show concrete demand. Copilot conversational behavior and one Muse shopper support assistance/delegation interest. Counterevidence: selected anecdotes and vendor outcomes do not establish ordinary consumer preference or net savings. Confidence medium for bounded work, low for general substitution. E-BG-013,023–027,034,036,038.
- Q003: Planning/tool use, persistent execution and standardized integrations enable cross-app work. State recovery, context overhead, GUI precision and prompt injection remain constraints. Counterevidence: historical benchmark failures and newer latency study. Confidence high on mechanisms, medium on generalization. E-BG-009–017,018–021,031.
- Q004: Subscription/bundled distribution and cheaper bounded work favor agents, while inference cost, integration and platform refusal favor limits. Counterevidence: enterprise profit has not tracked adoption and one shopper cannot prove a market shift. Confidence medium. E-BG-001–008,016–017,022–027,034,038.
- Q005: Scope-limited access, action approval, inspectable activity, correction/revocation and accountability are conditions visible in shipped design and policy guidance. Counterevidence: immutable individual memories and training/access gaps undermine simple privacy claims. Confidence high on documented controls, medium on effectiveness. E-BG-003–004,011,019,028–031.
- Q006: Unreliable repeat execution, latency, cost, platform blocking, opaque memory and product consolidation could stop dominance. Counterevidence to pessimism: real restricted rollouts and bounded deployments now exist. Confidence high that constraints exist, low on which wins. E-BG-001–004,009–017,021,031,034,037–038.
- Q007: Agent-first persistent assistants, agent-augmented incumbent surfaces and specialized bounded agents all fit the evidence. Dominance is not currently demonstrated. Counterevidence to a single forecast: provider handoff in Search, specialized case studies and Mariner consolidation. Confidence medium on coexistence, low on eventual share. E-BG-005–008,018–027,032–038.
- Q008: Research/planning and provider discovery are supported in official feature accounts. Muse advertises travel/form/payment actions with approvals, while Google Search explicitly hands final booking to providers. No retained source establishes reliable end-to-end family preference negotiation, all bookings, payment, disruption recovery and refunds. Counterevidence: tau-bench policy/repeatability and platform refusal. Confidence medium on individual advertised steps, low on whole journey. E-BG-010–012,019,022,030–031,033,038.

Unresolved child question candidates: What fraction of ordinary users repeatedly delegate purchases after failures? Which party reimburses an authorized but mistaken booking? How do household members resolve conflicting preferences and revoke previously shared context? These are gaps to investigate, not new factual findings.

## Source Ladder Findings (Rungs 1–10)

All sources were searched/opened fresh on 2026-10-10. FRESHNESS is publication date, never retrieval date. Canonical S IDs appear in every card with author/type/date, Q/E links, exact URL and retrieved refs; this is the source index embedded in the three-section contract. Same-URL cards share one S ID. Searches used no date filters; recent claims were verified in page bodies. Pages opened through search are records of retrieved source text, not independent physical product testing.

### Rung 1 — Consulting & Analyst

Coverage: 4/5 target strong findings; shortfall 1. Stopped at the quality floor after the logged bounded pass; missing independent outcomes or additional nonduplicate evidence remain coverage gaps, not evidence of absence.

| Q ID | rung | date | source channel/search used | pages checked | finding/null/blocked/relevance result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q004,Q006,Q007 | 1 | 2026-10-10 | McKinsey state-of-AI 2025/2026; Gartner cancellations/governance; official analyst press pages | McKinsey survey, Gartner cancellation and governance pages; supply-chain agent-washing page checked | Four retained distinct claims. Supply-chain recommendation was not retained as another general-agent finding. |

#### E-BG-001 — Scaling differs sharply by company size

SOURCE TYPE: Analyst
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: 2026-08-25
USE: Cite

McKinsey surveyed 1,719 respondents in 97 countries during May–June 2026, weighted by national GDP.

CLAIM: Large-company respondents scaling agents rose from 27% to 40%; smaller organizations remained at 22%.

WHY IT MATTERS: Organizational resources influence adoption; enterprise scale cannot establish consumer dominance.

CONFIDENCE: Medium

https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai

Question links: Q004,Q006,Q007

Source/provenance links: S-BG-001; retrieved turn12view10; access 2026-10-10. Self-report, not audited deployments; no consumer-agent denominator.

#### E-BG-002 — Growing use does not imply growing profit

SOURCE TYPE: Analyst
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: 2026-08-25
USE: Cite

The same McKinsey 2026 survey reports enterprise economics alongside adoption.

CLAIM: 37% reported positive AI contribution to EBIT, essentially unchanged; operating costs constrained use for about one in five organizations.

WHY IT MATTERS: Delegation must save more than inference, integration and oversight cost.

CONFIDENCE: Medium

https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai

Question links: Q004,Q006

Source/provenance links: S-BG-001; retrieved turn8view0; access 2026-10-10. Shared survey with previous card; distinct economic outcome, not independent corroboration.

#### E-BG-003 — Cancellation forecast has explicit causes

SOURCE TYPE: Analyst
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: 2025-06-25
USE: Cite

Gartner distinguishes genuine agents from rebranded automation in its press release.

CLAIM: Gartner forecasts over 40% of agentic projects will be canceled by end-2027 because of cost, unclear value or inadequate risk controls.

WHY IT MATTERS: A future thesis must survive economics and controls, not merely model improvement.

CONFIDENCE: Medium

https://www.gartner.com/en/newsroom/press-releases/2025-06-25-gartner-predicts-over-40-percent-of-agentic-ai-projects-will-be-canceled-by-end-of-2027

Question links: Q004,Q005,Q006

Source/provenance links: S-BG-002; retrieved turn8view1; access 2026-10-10. Forecast, not observed cancellation rate; webinar poll is a convenience sample.

#### E-BG-004 — Governance must distinguish access from autonomy

SOURCE TYPE: Analyst
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: 2026-05-26
USE: Cite

Gartner analyst Shiva Varma warns against treating every agent as either locked down or fully trusted.

CLAIM: Gartner identifies conflating ability to act with granted access as a failure mechanism and forecasts agent demotions following incidents.

WHY IT MATTERS: Readers need graduated permissions rather than a binary trust switch.

All personas are simulated analytic lenses, not real interviews or statements by employees. Selected after background38 cards.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P01 | Microsoft consumer Copilot product manager | Product scope, distribution, retention and competing surfaces | Q001,Q007,Q008 | Benefits from assistant growth; may confuse engagement with outcome completion | E-BG-018–022 show different availability and product surfaces; E-BG-037 challenges standalone inevitability |
| P02 | Booking.com senior UX researcher | Current traveler pain → proposed delegation → shape/form → validation | Q002,Q008,Q006 | Marketplace research can privilege existing booking flow and omit noncustomers | E-BG-033 provider handoff and E-BG-038 anecdote leave user preference and full holiday journey uncertain |
| P03 | Anthropic staff software/AI engineer | Tool execution, state recovery, benchmark transfer and evaluation | Q003,Q006 | Technical benchmarks may substitute for longitudinal real-world value | E-BG-009–017 explain technical enabling layers and constraints |
| P04 | Microsoft enterprise privacy counsel / governance lead | Scope, revocation, accountability, household/enterprise consent | Q005,Q008,Q006 | May overweight compliance and underweight practical usability | E-BG-030–031 expose data/memory boundaries despite broad autonomy claims |
| P05 | Expedia business-unit leader with economics remit | Market structure, transaction incentives, distribution, defensible agent economics | Q004,Q007 | Incumbent marketplace benefits from preserving provider handoff | E-BG-001–008 forecasts and E-BG-038 platform refusal suggest market access matters |
| P06 | Shopify senior product marketing manager | Adoption proposition, discoverability, trust messaging, differentiated value | Q002,Q004,Q007 | Benefits from agent checkout growth; campaign claims may exceed measured demand | E-BG-038 shopping narrative needs scrutiny distinct from demonstrated general demand |
| P07 | Salesforce enterprise sales / solutions lead | Buying criteria, deployments, measurable ROI, procurement, support | Q001,Q004,Q005 | Incentive to sell pilots as scalable outcomes | E-BG-024 pilot/ServiceCloud ROI attribution illustrates risk of conflation |

Four independent streams: EX01 P01; EX02 P02; EX03 P03+P04 with separate role-specific questions; EX04 P05+P06+P07 with separate role-specific questions. Adjacent-role grouping is scheduling, not one interchangeable persona. Competing incentives kept visible: platform orchestration vs merchant/marketplace transaction control, engineering capability vs UXR demonstrated benefit, sales growth vs privacy governance.

# Expert questions — EX01 — 2026-10-10

P01: Microsoft consumer Copilot product manager, simulated analytic lens. These are proposed research questions, not employee statements, interviews, research findings or conclusions. Read the frame, background, panel and canonical output contract; no retrieval performed.

| Q ID | Question | Parent Q | Why it matters | Perspective/owner | Evidence needed | Relevant rungs | E/S links | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q009 | Across currently accessible consumer Copilot and comparable personal-agent products, which consequential cross-app tasks can an ordinary eligible user complete with persistent context, and what access, approval or provider-handoff boundaries prevent completion? | Q001 | A platform capability inventory could substantially narrow the claim from general personal agents to a few bounded tasks, or establish that persistent delegation is already usable beyond demos. | P01 — product scope and access | Dated official availability and plan/region/device details; task-level documentation; reproducible independent attempts including blocked tasks; distinguish announcements, previews and shipped access. | 4,5,6,10 | E-BG-018/S-BG-017; E-BG-019/S-BG-018; E-BG-020/S-BG-019; E-BG-033/S-BG-032; E-BG-037/S-BG-036 | open |
| Q010 | In users with sustained access to a personal agent, does repeated delegated-action use reduce direct app operation across meaningful task categories, or does it mainly add an assistant entry point while apps retain execution and verification? | Q007 | Assistant engagement or bundled reach can coexist with continued app dependence. Measured substitution could support dominance; durable complementary use would support agent-augmented apps instead. | P01 — retention and competing surfaces | Longitudinal cohorts with eligible-user denominators, task categories, repeat successful outcomes, direct app use before/after, abandonment/reversion, oversight time and total time saved; independent measurements or transparent vendor methods. | 1,3,4,6,9,10 | E-BG-013/S-BG-012; E-BG-022/S-BG-021; E-BG-026/S-BG-025; E-BG-034/S-BG-033; E-BG-037/S-BG-036 | open |
| Q011 | For a multi-provider family holiday, can a shipped personal agent preserve the family's constraints through booking, payment and a later disruption or change, and at which points do provider access, approvals or recovery force people back into app interfaces? | Q008 | Successful discovery or an initial purchase may overstate replacement of the full journey. Post-booking continuity and recovery could change which parts of intent-to-outcome work are credible near-term. | P01 — journey completion and product boundaries | Dated task-by-task capability checks and observed authorized tests; explicit budget/preferences; booking confirmations and payment boundaries; cancellation/change/refund handling, state reconciliation, human interventions and failure recovery; compare provider handoff with agent completion. | 3,4,5,6,7,10 | E-BG-010/S-BG-009; E-BG-019/S-BG-018; E-BG-031/S-BG-030; E-BG-033/S-BG-032; E-BG-038/S-BG-037 | open |

## Purpose and dependencies

| Q ID | Purpose | Dependencies |
|---|---|---|
| Q009 | Establish the actual reachable product boundary before interpreting adoption or future direction; include Microsoft as a coverage need because the background retained no precise Microsoft shipping case. | Q001; task definitions and permission boundaries from Q003,Q005. |
| Q010 | Test behavioral substitution rather than infer dominance from chat traffic, subscriptions, bundling or review-agent volume. | Q009,Q002,Q004; feed the alternative futures comparison in Q007. |
| Q011 | Test continuity of an outcome across transactions and changed circumstances; retain the holiday as an analytical scenario unless observed evidence is found. | Q009,Q003,Q005; provider-access evidence from Q004; feed Q008 and Q007. |

Grounding is limited to the supplied background cards and their declared limitations. These limitations motivate questions rather than supply answers. No new evidence IDs allocated.

# P02 — Grounded expert questions — 2026-10-10

Perspective: Booking.com senior UX researcher, simulated analytic lens. This is question generation from the supplied frame, background and panel, not an interview, retrieval pass or finding about travelers. The family-holiday journey remains an analytical scenario. Existing booking-flow bias and omission of noncustomers are explicit sampling risks.

Required sequence: current pain → proposed agent solution → shape/form → validation. Each step depends on evidence from the preceding step; a plausible scenario or product announcement does not establish experienced pain or preferred delegation.

| Q ID | Question | Parent Q | Purpose | Why material | Grounding E IDs and limits | Evidence needed | Dependencies | Perspective/owner | Relevant rungs | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q012 | In observed holiday planning and change-handling, which tasks do travelers experience as burdensome, which do they value doing themselves, and how do these differ across household roles, trip complexity and users who do not book through marketplaces? | Q002 | Current pain: establish actual work, coping strategies and valued involvement before proposing delegation. | If the apparent burden is enjoyable exploration, interpersonal negotiation or a provider-policy problem, interface replacement may solve little; the chapter must distinguish those cases. | E-BG-013 shows context-dependent assistant conversation, not consequential delegation; E-BG-025 shows bounded organizational workload pain, not traveler pain; E-BG-038 is one interested shopper's account, not travel research. These are prompts for investigation, not observed holiday pain. | Contextual inquiry, diaries or recorded journeys from diverse travelers and household participants; actual time, switching, coordination and rework; examples of willing app use; noncustomer and accessibility perspectives. Separate reported frustration from observed behavior. | Q002 evidence base; no dependency on accepting the dominance thesis. | P02 | 3,4,6,9,10 | open |
| Q013 | For the burdens established in Q012, which bounded agent intervention—gathering options, maintaining a shared plan, preparing transactions or executing authorized changes—would users actually choose over their current method, and which judgments would they retain? | Q008 | Proposed agent solution: translate a verified need into a testable delegation proposition and define its boundaries. | Intent expression can shift effort into specifying constraints and reviewing actions; discovering preferred delegation boundaries could favor assistance, specialist agents or persistent orchestration. | E-BG-019 describes approvals and advertised travel actions without independent completion rates; E-BG-033 retains provider booking handoff; E-BG-027 illustrates the need for live state and policy context in a different domain. None demonstrates traveler preference for the proposed intervention. | Need-linked concept tests and behavioral choice tasks with working prototypes; verification of available travel actions, price/availability state and provider-policy constraints; household accounts of conflicting preferences; decisions to delegate or refuse at each step. | Q012; Q001 shipped scope; Q003 execution feasibility; Q005 consent boundaries. | P02 | 3,4,5,6,7,10 | open |
| Q014 | For the delegation boundaries identified in Q013, which interaction form best lets travelers express and revise constraints, compare tradeoffs, authorize spending and recover from changes: persistent conversational agent, visual itinerary with agent actions, or agent-assisted provider handoff? | Q008 | Shape/form: compare concrete interfaces after establishing need and intervention. | A useful agent may require visual comparison and specialist transaction surfaces; preferred form determines whether the hypothesis concerns replacing apps or changing how apps are operated. | E-BG-019 offers app/WhatsApp access with approvals; E-BG-033 demonstrates a provider-handoff design; E-BG-030–031 expose data-use and memory-correction boundaries. These documented designs do not establish usability or preference. | Comparative usability studies with equivalent tasks and capabilities; comprehension of itinerary, total cost, cancellation rules and authorization; ability to correct or revoke preferences; recovery from changed dates and conflicting household requests; accessibility testing. | Q012–Q013; Q005 usable permissions; Q001 capability parity across compared forms. | P02 | 3,4,5,7,10 | open |
| Q015 | In repeated real or safely simulated planning, booking and disruption tasks, does the best-performing form from Q014 improve successful outcomes and total human effort enough to produce sustained voluntary delegation, including after errors, compared with existing apps and simpler assistance? | Q006 | Validation: test the proposed benefit and a behavioral falsifier for dominance. | Satisfaction or one impressive completion cannot establish replacement; persistent checking, rework, loss of control or return to apps could weaken the thesis despite technically successful actions. | E-BG-010 concerns historical simulated-task repeatability; E-BG-012 concerns measured computer-use trajectory efficiency, not traveler net effort; E-BG-034 does not supply agent retention; E-BG-038 is one shopper's self-report. Their limits motivate longitudinal validation rather than predict its result. | Preregistered comparative study with representative cohorts, equivalent capabilities and repeated tasks; count setup, specification, approval, monitoring and recovery effort; independently verify outcome correctness, unexpected costs and policy compliance; record voluntary reuse, abandonment and app return after failures. For rare holidays, combine safe task repetitions with subsequent-trip follow-up and distinguish intention from actual reuse. | Q012–Q014; Q001 verified scope; Q003 recovery; Q005 consent and accountability; Q006 competing explanations. | P02 | 1,3,4,6,7,9,10 | open |

Handoff: four open child questions, Q012–Q015. No new E IDs, searches, conclusions or claims of observed traveler pain. Research should preserve the sequence and include dissenting users; a narrower useful product is a legitimate result, not evidence of general agent dominance.

# Personal agents — grounded expert questions EX03

As of: 2026-10-10. Question generation only; no new retrieval or conclusions. Grounded in the supplied frame, background and panel; evidence pointers below refer to supplied background cards. P03 and P04 are distinct simulated company-role perspectives, not interviews or statements by employees. Audience: broad Products of Tomorrow readership, including personal and professional products. Agent dominance remains a testable hypothesis.

## Child Question Map

| Q ID | Question | Parent Q | Why it matters / role-specific purpose | Perspective/owner | Evidence needed | Relevant rungs | E/S grounding | Dependencies | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q016 | For a cross-app task that changes real state, can a personal agent detect partial completion, resume after interruption and prevent duplicate or contradictory actions with less total user work than a conventional app workflow? | Q003 | P03 tests the engineering transition from a plausible plan to a reliable outcome. A flight purchase followed by a failed hotel reservation makes recovery and state verification central; simple task success cannot establish the advantage. If recovery requires substantial manual reconciliation, agents may remain useful orchestration layers while apps retain the dependable transaction boundary. | P03 — simulated Anthropic staff software/AI engineer | Current execution-based comparisons on the same tasks, repeated runs, deliberately injected tool failures/session loss, independently verified external state, duplicate-action rates and recovery time. Count user checking, correction and intervention along with agent runtime. Seek consumer and professional tasks; do not generalize one architecture's token ratios. | 3,4,5,6,10 | E-BG-010 / S-BG-009: repeated reliability differs from single success; E-BG-015 / S-BG-014: connection is not correctness; E-BG-016 / S-BG-015: resumable execution is an engineering requirement. These motivate a test, not a measured current recovery failure rate. | Q001 supplies shipped task boundaries; Q003 supplies mechanisms; Q005 supplies authority limits; Q008 provides the holiday example without asserting observed need. | open |
| Q017 | Do gains on current computer-use benchmarks predict repeated safe completion and net time savings in messy real-world cross-app work, or do agents still depend on users performing enough verification to preserve app-led working? | Q006 | P03 challenges the transfer from technical performance to dominance. This question could change the thesis even if benchmarks improve: an agent can complete a task while consuming more user attention or creating uncertain outcomes. It separates a research capability signal from a replacement proposition. | P03 — simulated Anthropic staff software/AI engineer | Dated benchmark/model versions and prospective field studies of those same systems on representative workflows. Measure repeatability, policy violations, latency, user verification/correction time and consequential errors against human app use; include task selection, dropout and unsuccessful attempts. Compare inspectable coding artifacts with transactions whose errors are harder to test. | 3,4,6,9,10 | E-BG-009 / S-BG-008: historical execution gap, not current frontier performance; E-BG-010 / S-BG-009: historical repeatability limit; E-BG-012 / S-BG-011: extra steps and latency; E-BG-036 / S-BG-035: selected engineers retain artifact inspection. | Q001 identifies current systems; Q002 establishes actual workflows and user behavior; Q016 identifies recovery costs to include. | open |
| Q018 | Can a user meaningfully inspect, correct and revoke an agent's remembered preferences and connected-service access, including data already integrated into context or downstream systems, without having to abandon the agent entirely? | Q005 | P04 tests whether persistent personalization supports usable, continuing consent. The mechanism that makes a personal agent valuable can also make permissions difficult to reverse. If granular correction and revocation are unavailable or confusing, broad persistent delegation may lose to task-specific agents or app permissions users understand. | P04 — simulated Microsoft enterprise privacy counsel / governance lead | Current product controls and data-flow documentation, independent tests of correction/deletion/disconnection, retention and onward-use boundaries, and usability studies showing whether users understand the consequences. Identify jurisdiction and distinguish legal duties from voluntary frameworks and company promises. Test household and enterprise shared-data cases as well as single-user memory. | 3,4,5,7,10 | E-BG-031 / S-BG-030: disconnection differs from deleting integrated context and individual memories cannot currently be modified; E-BG-030 / S-BG-029: several data-use boundaries and future protection promises; E-BG-028 / S-BG-027: lifecycle risk management is voluntary, not certification. | Q001 establishes current controls; Q003 explains memory/tool propagation; Q006 assesses whether burdens alter adoption. | open |
| Q019 | When a personal agent arranges and later changes a family holiday, whose authorization is required for shared personal data, nonrefundable spending and changes affecting other travelers, and who can correct or compensate an erroneous action? | Q008 | P04 locates the boundary between one person's intent and a legitimate household outcome. The illustrative journey contains several people's interests and separate suppliers' rules; one broad instruction may not authorize every action. If usable approvals, dispute handling and redress require repeated supplier interfaces, the likely future may preserve app handoffs even with capable planning. | P04 — simulated Microsoft enterprise privacy counsel / governance lead | Current verified authorization/payment/change flows; supplier and agent terms allocating responsibility; applicable privacy, payment and consumer-protection requirements with jurisdiction/date; tests involving conflicting traveler preferences, minors' data and cancellation errors; documented incidents or redress outcomes. Separate required approvals from engineering safeguards, and count how much interface work remains. | 3,4,5,6,7,10 | E-BG-011 / S-BG-010: untrusted tool content can conflict with delegated intent, without establishing live incidence; E-BG-030 / S-BG-029: household-sensitive information crosses data-use boundaries; E-BG-033 / S-BG-032: Google supplies provider links to complete booking; E-BG-038 / S-BG-037: one shopper's experience illustrates merchant access restrictions, not mass behavior. | Q001 supplies shipped journey steps; Q002 establishes whether families want this delegation; Q005 and Q018 supply consent/revocation conditions; Q016 supplies recoverability. | open |

| Q020 | Under what commercial terms would travel providers and marketplaces let an independent personal agent complete bookings and handle subsequent changes, and would those terms leave the agent economically viable compared with provider handoff? | Q004 | Tests whether intent-to-outcome travel can survive the incentives around commissions, customer ownership, servicing and liability. If commercially rational access ends at discovery, the thesis may shift toward agent-assisted apps even when models can execute transactions. | P05 — Expedia business-unit leader with economics remit; incumbent incentive to preserve distribution and servicing control remains visible | Actual agent-access agreements or documented restrictions; commission and referral arrangements; transaction completion and repeat-booking data; total execution, support, refund and dispute cost per successful booking; comparisons with conventional and agent-assisted provider journeys. Separate signed terms and measured results from proposed business models. | 1,2,4,5,6,7,10 | E-BG-002 operating-cost constraint; E-BG-005 marketplace forecast; E-BG-033 provider completion links; E-BG-038 platform refusal in one shopper's account. The last two concern product handoff and shopping respectively, not proof of travel-market economics. | Q001 establishes shipped access; Q003 establishes execution/recovery capabilities; Q005 establishes authority and liability boundaries; feeds Q007 and the Q008 holiday scenario. | open |
| Q021 | Among shoppers offered agent checkout, which specific burdens make them repeatedly delegate purchases, and does observed repeat use replace their usual store or app journeys or mainly add another discovery channel? | Q002 | Tests the difference between a compelling adoption proposition and behavioral substitution. Repeated delegation across routine and consequential purchases would support the dominance hypothesis; occasional niche purchases or abandoned delegation would narrow it. | P06 — Shopify senior product marketing manager; incentive to present agent checkout as growth is explicit | Longitudinal cohorts with clear eligible-user denominators; matched or experimental comparisons of agent checkout, assisted discovery and normal shopping; interviews grounded in observed shopping sessions; time saved, correction burden, conversion, returns and repeat use; separation of new transactions from displaced journeys. | Q022 | When buyers can choose between a cross-app personal work agent and bounded agents inside existing enterprise products, do measured net returns and required permission, audit and recovery controls favor one approach after pilots become sustained deployments? | Q005 | Tests whether accountability conditions make broad delegation practical or pull products back toward bounded workflows. It could change the professional half of the thesis if broad agents remain appealing demonstrations while specialist agents deliver more reliable value under real procurement and support constraints. | P07 — Salesforce enterprise sales / solutions lead; incentive to convert pilots and attribute platform ROI to agents remains explicit | Documented buying criteria and deployment decisions; comparable post-pilot cohorts; agent-specific cost and benefit attribution including integration, oversight, compliance and error recovery; permission and audit requirements; cancellation or scope-reduction records; buyer and operator accounts independent of sales materials. # Personal agents — Expert Research EX01 — 2026-10-10

## Perspective & Assigned Questions

P01 is a simulated Microsoft consumer Copilot product-manager lens, not an interview or a statement by Microsoft employees. It examines reachable product scope, persistence and behavioral substitution. Its incentive is assistant growth; its blind spot is confusing engagement with completed outcomes. The Products of Tomorrow frame includes both personal and professional use, with dominance treated as a hypothesis. The family holiday remains an analytical scenario.

Assigned questions, preserved verbatim:

- **Q009:** Across currently accessible consumer Copilot and comparable personal-agent products, which consequential cross-app tasks can an ordinary eligible user complete with persistent context, and what access, approval or provider-handoff boundaries prevent completion?
- **Q010:** In users with sustained access to a personal agent, does repeated delegated-action use reduce direct app operation across meaningful task categories, or does it mainly add an assistant entry point while apps retain execution and verification?
- **Q011:** For a multi-provider family holiday, can a shipped personal agent preserve the family's constraints through booking, payment and a later disruption or change, and at which points do provider access, approvals or recovery force people back into app interfaces?

Broader questions Q001,Q007,Q008 are addressed below. All supplied frame, background, panel, expanded map and question artifacts, research skill and canonical output contract were read. Fresh public source bodies were opened; no transaction, account-access test or field experiment was performed. A progressive checkpoint was saved after core retrieval and replaced with this complete bounded brief.

## Question Answers

### Q009 — Partial

**Answer:** Microsoft documents meaningful consumer delegation, but access and product continuity are specific. Browse with Copilot runs visible browser actions in Edge for US Microsoft 365 Premium consumers. Tasks documents browser/connector execution and scheduling; its current support scope includes personal accounts with Personal, Family, Premium and Pro subscriptions. These establish documented functionality, not verified success for every eligible account. Tasks is migrating to Cowork Preview, with material capability and entitlement changes. E-EX01-003–005 / S-EX01-003–005 support this boundary.

Comparable products remain heterogeneous: E-BG-018 / S-BG-017 describes dots rollout, E-BG-019 / S-BG-018 Muse rollout and approvals, and E-BG-021 / S-BG-020 Claude computer-use research preview. Existing background evidence remains unchanged. See its original linked URLs; those three sources were not independently re-opened in this bounded Microsoft-focused pass. The September25 Microsoft announcement does not establish general consumer access to Autopilot: it describes private preview and tenant identity. Code consumer preview was still scheduled for later in 2026 (S-EX01-007).

**Counterevidence:** Execution is real documented product behavior, so a blanket claim that personal agents are only demos is too strong. Conversely, experimental access, supervision and migration losses undermine the assumption of a stable universal assistant.

**Confidence:** High in documented Microsoft boundaries; medium in a cross-product inventory. **Limits:** No account-level eligibility audit, task success rate or independent reproducible completion test. **Status:** partial.

Sources: [Tasks](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-copilot/using-copilot-tasks), [Browse](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-copilot/browse-with-copilot), [migration](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-copilot/learning/changes-microsoft-copilot-app), [September announcement](https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/09/25/introducing-the-new-copilot-with-home-code-and-autopilot/).

### Q010 — Open

**Answer:** No retrieved source measures sustained delegated outcomes together with reduced direct app use across meaningful categories. E-EX01-002 / S-EX01-002 is longitudinal, but studies conversational behavior in 2024. It supplies a warning against inferring individual behavioral change from aggregate growth, not an agent-displacement result. E-EX01-001 measures stated willingness; E-EX01-006 supplies selected reversals. Neither resolves substitution.

The fresh a16z October5 edition (S-EX01-008) discusses early agent users and vendor-reported transaction scale, but provides no transparent before/after direct-app usage series. Its investment incentives and selected early-user channels limit representativeness. Background E-BG-013, E-BG-026 and E-BG-034 likewise measure conversations, a bounded review workflow or assistant subscriptions rather than broad displacement.

**Counterevidence:** E-BG-038 describes one user's purchasing substitution, and the new investor review reports consequential transaction activity. These keep agent-first behavior plausible for some users. They do not establish durable population dominance. Selected reversals also cannot establish general failure.

**Confidence:** High that this retrieved set does not answer the question; low in any direction-of-substitution conclusion. **Limits:** This is a bounded search, not proof that no private or public cohort exists. **Status:** open.

Sources: [longitudinal paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29018), [Gartner survey](https://www.gartner.com/en/newsroom/press-releases/2026-05-27-gartner-survey-finds-consumers-want-ai-shopping-help-but-not-ai-purchase-decisions), [investor review](https://a16z.com/100-gen-ai-apps-7/), [selected reversals](https://www.businessinsider.com/early-users-delete-personal-ai-agents-privacy-scares-blunders-2026-10).

### Q011 — Partial

**Answer:** Current documentation supports searching, preparing and supervised browser action, with payment/personal-data approval or takeover boundaries. It does not demonstrate a single agent retaining a family's constraints across multiple confirmed bookings, subsequent disruption, cancellation/refund and reconciled external state. E-EX01-003–004 describe those execution boundaries. E-EX01-005 establishes that product migration itself can interrupt persistence; it is not a measured holiday failure.

A named travel partner, Skyscanner, documented flight comparison prompts at the historical US Labs launch (S-EX01-009). It does not prove ticket purchase, servicing or disruption recovery. E-BG-033 / S-BG-032 supplies Google's provider completion links, and E-BG-019 advertises Muse travel actions with approvals; neither is an observed full family lifecycle. E-BG-010 is historical simulated reliability research, not current travel completion performance.

**Counterevidence:** Generic browser execution and task-specific memory could technically support portions of the journey. Absence of a named holiday case does not prove impossibility. A planning example or partner list also cannot establish completion.

**Confidence:** High in identified documentary boundaries; low in full-journey capability or preservation claims. **Limits:** No safely authorized live test; no independently confirmed bookings or servicing records. **Status:** partial.

Sources: [Tasks](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-copilot/using-copilot-tasks), [Browse](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-copilot/browse-with-copilot), [migration](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-copilot/learning/changes-microsoft-copilot-app), [Skyscanner](https://www.partners.skyscanner.net/news-case-studies/microsoft-copilot-partnership).

## Source Ladder Findings (Rungs 1–10)

### 1. Consulting & Analyst

**E-EX01-001 — Consumers distinguish shopping help from delegated purchase decisions**

SOURCE TYPE: Analyst
AUTHORITY: Medium
FRESHNESS: 2026-05-27
USE: Cite

Gartner's January2026 survey of 322 US consumers separates shopping assistance from willingness to let AI choose purchases. Its public release gives only limited methodological detail.

CLAIM: Willingness to let AI make purchase decisions peaked at 11% in lower-stakes categories, versus 31% willing to let it narrow household-supply choices.
WHY IT MATTERS: Choosing discovery help cannot be counted as demand for consequential autonomous action.
CONFIDENCE: Medium
https://www.gartner.com/en/newsroom/press-releases/2026-05-27-gartner-survey-finds-consumers-want-ai-shopping-help-but-not-ai-purchase-decisions
Question links: Q010,Q007
Source/provenance links: S-EX01-001; original survey release, not observed behavior or a representative agent-user retention cohort.

### 2. VC / Investor Research

No new card. a16z edition7 opened: useful early-user signal, not direct-app displacement evidence. S-EX01-008 supplements, rather than updates or replaces, E-BG-034's original dated assessment. Investor incentives and company-reported volumes remain visible.

### 3. Academic / Research

**E-EX01-002 — Aggregate conversational growth does not imply changing individual habits**

SOURCE TYPE: Academic
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: 2026-05-27; revised 2026-08-31
USE: Cite

Rebecca M. M. Hicke and Kiran Tomlinson analyze approximately12,000 randomly sampled Bing Copilot-user trajectories and compare WildChat. The opened full paper identifies the Copilot observation window as January–September2024; publication freshness is not behavioral freshness.

CLAIM: The study finds weaker changes within individual trajectories than population trends and substantial persistence of user habits.
WHY IT MATTERS: Adoption counts cannot substitute for tracking what the same people stop operating themselves.
CONFIDENCE: High
https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29018
Question links: Q010,Q007
Source/provenance links: S-EX01-002; full v2 body https://arxiv.org/html/2605.29018v2 opened. Microsoft-affiliated observational research; conversations, not current delegated-action outcomes or app-use measurement.

### 4. Practitioner & Product

**E-EX01-003 — Copilot Tasks delegates work but retains approval and site-access boundaries**

SOURCE TYPE: Primary
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: unknown
USE: Cite

Microsoft Support describes immediate or scheduled goal execution through browsers and authorized connectors, with task memory. The living page applies to personal accounts on Personal, Family, Premium and Pro, while flagging migration.

CLAIM: Tasks requires approval or user completion for sensitive actions and may hand control back when sites restrict automation or require manual interaction.
WHY IT MATTERS: An agent may reduce navigation while users still handle consequential commitments and blocked providers.
CONFIDENCE: High
https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-copilot/using-copilot-tasks
Question links: Q009,Q011,Q001,Q008
Source/provenance links: S-EX01-003; company documentation, no demonstrated success rate; publication date unavailable and eligibility can change.

### 5. Big Platforms & Major Products

**E-EX01-004 — Consumer browser action is available inside Edge with narrow rollout and supervision**

SOURCE TYPE: Primary
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: unknown
USE: Cite

Microsoft's Browse with Copilot support page describes local, visible Edge interaction. Sean Lyndersay's dated May13 Edge announcement independently confirms the company's transition from limited-preview Copilot Actions to this named offering; both are Microsoft sources, not independent corroboration.

CLAIM: Browse with Copilot is rolling out to US Microsoft 365 Premium consumers, and purchases/reservations require attention or supervision; personal/payment details can require takeover.
WHY IT MATTERS: Intent interaction is shipping inside an existing browser, with app-based transaction surfaces and user control still present.
CONFIDENCE: High
https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-copilot/browse-with-copilot
Question links: Q009,Q011,Q001,Q007,Q008
Source/provenance links: S-EX01-004; experimental feature, no completion measurement; dated corroborating company source S-EX01-010, https://blogs.windows.com/msedgedev/2026/05/13/new-updates-to-edge-across-desktop-and-mobile/ (2026-05-13), usage limits apply.

Official blog, executive voice and roadmap channels were all attempted. S-EX01-007's September25 announcement explicitly separates Frontier/private previews from later consumer Code preview. S-EX01-011's April4 2025 Suleyman vision describes the companion direction; it is not evidence of current access or demand. Existing platform background cards retain their IDs.

### 6. Case Studies

No additional case card: named Skyscanner partnership body (S-EX01-009) verifies launch participation and flight-comparison examples, but offers no full holiday servicing outcome. Lifecycle discontinuity below is a product-documentation finding, not a named user case. Independent user reversals are retained under Rung10.

### 7. Ethics / Regulatory

No new regulatory card. Current consumer privacy-controls documentation S-EX01-012 was opened and assessed against the persistent-context assumption. It documents saved-memory deletion and distinguishes disabling memory from deleting it. This is a company control description, not an independent usability audit or legal approval. Approval/site boundaries are already E-EX01-003–004; do not count them twice.

### 8. Trend & Futures

**E-EX01-005 — Tasks migration changes access and breaks some persistence**

SOURCE TYPE: Primary
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: unknown
USE: Cite

Microsoft Support's current app-updates page says personal-account Tasks customers will begin moving to Cowork Preview over the next few weeks. The undated transition notice is a forward migration statement, not proof every account has moved.

CLAIM: Migrated Tasks memories will not transfer, schedules arrive disabled, and Personal/Family subscribers lose runnable access unless upgrading to Premium/Pro.
WHY IT MATTERS: Continuity depends on service lifecycle and commercial entitlement as well as model memory; an ongoing holiday plan could require re-establishing context and automation after a product transition.
CONFIDENCE: High
https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365-copilot/learning/changes-microsoft-copilot-app
Question links: Q009,Q010,Q011,Q001,Q007,Q008
Source/provenance links: S-EX01-005; explicit documented migration limits, not observed user losses. Page also identifies remote browsing as coming soon, with local Edge available; this does not imply all agent browsing was always local.

## Handoff

**Assigned statuses:** Q009 partial; Q010 open; Q011 partial. Broad Q001,Q007,Q008 remain partial. New E-EX01-001–006 and S-EX01-001–013. All ten rungs attempted; no50-card quota applied. Six cards checked for original fields, date honesty, Q links, canonical URL and duplication.

The most chapter-relevant addition is service-lifecycle continuity: remembering preferences is insufficient if migration drops task memories or changes runnable access. This is a sourced product boundary, while any implication for a particular holiday is explicitly analytical. Present Microsoft as shipping supervised consumer action with constrained reach, while stronger always-on work claims have preview boundaries. Do not convert the documented controls or roadmaps into reliability or demand claims.

**Unresolved needs:** Q010 needs independent longitudinal eligible-user cohorts measuring completed actions, direct app use, total oversight and reversion. Q011 needs independently verified, authorized multi-provider lifecycle trials, including changes/refunds and state reconciliation. Q009 needs account/region/device-level confirmation during migration and after completion; a support page scope is not an eligibility census.

**Candidate child question (unallocated):** What continuity guarantees or portable state does a personal agent offer when features, subscriptions or providers change? Parent Q009/Q011; grounded E-EX01-005. This could be tested by a documented migration audit rather than inventing traveler demand.

**Conflicts to retain:** Current Tasks page includes broader personal-plan scope while the migration page narrows future runnable scope. These are transition states, not automatically contradictory facts. Microsoft's consumer privacy page describes import of browser payment/password data, while Browse's Edge automation page restricts access to Edge autofill/wallet; different surfaces must not be flattened into a universal permission claim. a16z's early growth signals and selected user reversals concern different populations and metrics. Neither resolves dominance.

# Personal agents — Expert Research EX02 — 2026-10-10

## Perspective & Assigned Questions

P02: simulated Booking.com senior UX researcher lens; no interview or employee endorsement. Scope is Products of Tomorrow's broad audience and GenAI-enabled intent-to-outcome work. Dominance remains a hypothesis. Read frame-initial.md, background.md, panel.md, expanded-map.md, questions-ex02.md and the canonical research contracts. Marketplace bias and omission of noncustomers remain sampling risks. Assigned Q012–Q015, ordered pain → intervention → form → validation, under Q002,Q008,Q006. Evidence as of 2026-10-10; no researcher-conducted prototype or field test.

## Question Answers

### Q012 — Current work and valued involvement

Answer: Comparison and carrying context between sessions are evidenced work, rather than invented scenario pain (E-EX02-001/S-EX02-001). Tracked browsing supplies a separate behavioral baseline (E-EX02-003/S-EX02-002), but time online is not automatically wasted time. Retaining final choice matters (E-EX02-002). Household role, complexity, accessibility and marketplace-noncustomer differences remain unestablished.

Counterevidence: Exploration may have intrinsic value; a large browsing footprint alone cannot tell whether the traveler wants it removed. Self-reported diaries do not equal direct observation.

Confidence: Medium for evidenced comparison/context work; low for subgroup differences. Status: partial. Sources: https://www.mckinsey.com/industries/travel/our-insights/winning-hearts-in-an-age-of-infinite-travel-choices ; https://partner.expediagroup.com/en-us/resources/blog/path-to-purchase-insights

### Q013 — Need-linked intervention and retained judgment

Answer: An inspectable shortlist and resumable plan are testable responses to Q012, not proven preferred interventions. The autonomy gradient in E-EX02-002 suggests starting with assistance and explicit transaction choice. E-EX02-005 documents a bounded hybrid product; E-EX02-006 documents additional features. Neither proves travelers choose autonomous booking over their current method. E-BG-033/S-BG-032 already documents provider handoff; it is cross-referenced, not counted again.

Counterevidence: Product availability can precede demand. Constraints may be unresolved between people rather than missing from a search form, so expressing one person's intent need not yield a household outcome.

Confidence: Medium for a plausible bounded hypothesis, low for behavioral choice. Status: partial; preferred delegation intervention remains open. Sources: https://news.booking.com/bookingcom-launches-new-ai-trip-planner-to-enhance-travel-planning-experience/ ; https://openai.com/index/booking-com/ ; McKinsey URL above; background provider source https://blog.google/products-and-platforms/products/search/search-io-2026/

### Q014 — Interaction form

Answer: No retrieved capability-matched comparison establishes a winner. A visual plan with conversational edits is a concrete test candidate: E-EX02-005 documents chat plus listings; E-EX02-004 supplies behavioral data from another hybrid product. This is an analytic design implication, not a demonstrated visual-interface preference. A persistent conversational agent, visual itinerary with actions and assisted provider handoff must be compared on equivalent capability, price/policy comprehension, correction, spending approval and recovery.

Counterevidence: A commercial design may reflect incumbent distribution incentives. Tern varies tone, not interface form. No retrieved study answers whether a visually impaired traveler or a complex household benefits from the same design.

Confidence: High that a comparative winner is unestablished in this pass; low on which form wins. Status: open. Sources: Booking launch URL above; https://arxiv.org/html/2509.14259v1

### Q015 — Repeated benefit and voluntary delegation

Answer: Not established. E-EX02-004 contains actual behavior, but subscription/link-click outcomes do not establish successful bookings, saved total effort or subsequent-trip reuse after errors. E-EX02-006 supplies vendor outcome assertions without an auditable comparator. E-BG-010/S-BG-009 and E-BG-012/S-BG-011 motivate repeatability/effort measurement but do not answer the traveler question.

Counterevidence to dismissal: Some users pay for planning assistance, and bounded commercial features exist. Counterevidence to dominance: assistance adoption can coexist with continued app transaction control. Neither is a longitudinal substitution result.

Confidence: Low on durable net benefit; high on the distinction between the measured proxies and the requested outcome. Status: open. Sources: https://arxiv.org/html/2509.14259v1 ; https://openai.com/index/booking-com/ ; prior benchmark sources https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12045 and https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.16042v2 (prior IDs, not freshly verified performance here).

Proposed validation, not a conducted study: include planning nonusers and unsuccessful attempts; compare equivalent capable forms against existing apps and simpler assistance. Count specification, approvals, monitoring, correction and recovery. Verify dates, total costs, cancellation rules and confirmations separately from satisfaction. Follow actual subsequent trips and voluntary reuse; repeated safe tasks are a complement, not a substitute for real-trip follow-up. A product that completes actions but leaves total human effort unchanged or increases app return after mistakes would weaken dominance.

## Source Ladder Findings (Rungs 1–10)

Six genuine new cards across five canonical sources. Same-source cards are not independent corroboration. FRESHNESS means publication date; all access dates 2026-10-10. No 50-card quota recreated.

### Rung 1 — Consulting & Analyst

Two retained findings; same underlying study. Diary-based behavior is self-reported; attitudes are not revealed delegation choices.

#### E-EX02-001 — Comparison and iterative work have direct research grounding
SOURCE TYPE: Analyst
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: 2026-09-22
USE: Cite

McKinsey's Travel Practice reports a US survey exceeding 1,000 travelers plus 26 diary participants covering 211 actual-trip sessions.

CLAIM: Half report comparison across sources as their greatest frustration; approximately one-third of diary stage transitions moved back toward research or inspiration.

WHY IT MATTERS: Provides need evidence for preserving context without assuming all exploration is unwanted.

CONFIDENCE: Medium

https://www.mckinsey.com/industries/travel/our-insights/winning-hearts-in-an-age-of-infinite-travel-choices

Question links: Q012,Q013,Q002
Source/provenance links: S-EX02-001; Travel Practice authors Alex Gersovitz et al.; turn20view0/turn29view3. Self-report, not recorded household observation; methods do not establish subgroup causality. The body conflicts on accommodation changes (half versus four in ten); that statistic is excluded.

#### E-EX02-002 — Comfort declines at consequential action
SOURCE TYPE: Analyst
AUTHORITY: High
FRESHNESS: 2026-09-22
USE: Cite

The same McKinsey survey distinguishes stages of AI involvement.

CLAIM: Three-quarters prefer making the final booking themselves, despite greater comfort with AI inspiration and comparison.

WHY IT MATTERS: Transaction control challenges an inference from assistance interest to autonomous demand.

CONFIDENCE: Medium

https://www.mckinsey.com/industries/travel/our-insights/winning-hearts-in-an-age-of-infinite-travel-choices

Question links: Q013,Q014,Q015,Q006,Q008
Source/provenance links: S-EX02-001; turn20view0. Stated preference; not a behavioral experiment or universal prohibition on delegation.

No new retained card. a16z's travel opportunity essay was opened (turn20view1): useful historical forecast, not independent evidence of experienced pain or repeated benefit. https://a16z.com/adventure-is-out-there-opportunities-in-ai-x-travel/ . Publication date not verified in visible retrieved body. General claims about future autonomy were not used as current capability.

### Rung 3 — Academic / Research

One retained field-study finding. AlterAtlas HTML and abstract both returned internal errors; promising snippet-only prototype result excluded. GUI-versus-chat study discovered but not body-verified or counted.

#### E-EX02-004 — Hybrid planning has measured use, but action retention is unmeasured
SOURCE TYPE: Academic
AUTHORITY: Medium
FRESHNESS: 2025-09-12
USE: Cite

Lynna Jirpongopas and colleagues report a randomized tone experiment on Tern.app with an itinerary/map interface and optional conversational editing. Collection ran November 2024–April 2025; this is a preprint.

CLAIM: Of 1,617 itinerary-generating users, 282 chatted; among these, reported subscribers numbered 13/86 enthusiastic, 15/105 neutral and 5/91 control.

WHY IT MATTERS: Actual use and payment provide stronger evidence than intention, while optional-chat uptake cautions against assuming everyone wants conversation.

CONFIDENCE: Medium

https://arxiv.org/html/2509.14259v1

Question links: Q013,Q014,Q015,Q002,Q006
Source/provenance links: S-EX02-003; turn26view1/turn29view1/turn29view2. Outcomes concern planning subscriptions and affiliate clicks, not verified travel purchases or longitudinal delegation. Self-selection into chat restricts inference. Authors state users were not explicitly informed of the experiment. No independent replication retrieved; experiment does not compare interface forms.

### Rung 4 — Practitioner & Product

One retained behavioral baseline, with commissioned-research incentives stated. Product-engineering accounts in E-EX02-006 are cross-references, not new cards here.

#### E-EX02-003 — Browsing spans more than one booking channel
SOURCE TYPE: Primary
AUTHORITY: Medium
FRESHNESS: 2024-01-09
USE: Cite

Expedia's Cheryl Miller describes commissioned Luth Research using digital tracking from a 70,000-plus panel and surveys exceeding 5,700 respondents across seven markets.

CLAIM: Tracked travelers averaged 141 travel-content pages and 303 minutes in the 45 days preceding booking.

WHY IT MATTERS: Grounds work volume in behavior; it does not tell which minutes travelers would delegate.

CONFIDENCE: Medium

https://partner.expediagroup.com/en-us/resources/blog/path-to-purchase-insights

Question links: Q012,Q015,Q002
Source/provenance links: S-EX02-002; turn26view2; author senior VP B2B marketing. Panel is not equivalent to all travelers or the analyzed-trip denominator. Vendor summary, not raw data; historical observation predates current agents. Report promotes OTA distribution; no new causal pain inference.

### Rung 5 — Big Platforms & Major Products

Official launch body and CEO/CTO voice checked; roadmap language checked in current case source and later Booking release. One retained historical design finding; availability is not assumed unchanged in 2026.

#### E-EX02-005 — Booking combined conversation with visual choice and its app
SOURCE TYPE: Primary

USE: Cite

Booking.com's beta announcement describes US Genius-member access beginning June 28, 2023, with English app settings and sign-in.

CLAIM: Its planner presented visual property/destination lists with pricing and deep links, allowed switching between chat and app, and routed accommodation reservation through the app.

https://news.booking.com/bookingcom-launches-new-ai-trip-planner-to-enhance-travel-planning-experience/

### Rung 6 — Case Studies

https://openai.com/index/booking-com/

## Handoff

Q012 partial, Q013 partial, Q014 open, Q015 open. Six new cards E-EX02-001–006 and five sources S-EX02-001–005. Practical chapter implication, explicitly inference: the near-term opportunity may be continuity of traveler decisions with inspectable bounded assistance; current evidence does not select conversational autonomy as the universal replacement surface.

Unresolved: household preference negotiation and planner role differences; noncustomer/accessibility samples; actual choice of intervention; capability-matched interface comparison; independently measured repeat success, net effort and app substitution after errors; complete booking/change/refund scope. No new child Q ID needed: these remain within Q012–Q015. Candidate narrower child only if planner splits scope: “Does preserving rejected options and prior comparisons reduce repeated search without reducing valued exploration?”

Next evidence need: representative contextual inquiry followed by comparative tests and actual subsequent-trip follow-up. Keep stronger tracked behavior separate from reported frustration and vendor assertions; preserve the sequence pain → intervention → form → benefit. Missing accessible studies are research gaps, not proof of no demand. All-ten-rung bounded pass complete; no posting, chapter writing or new subagents.

# Personal agents — Expert research EX03 — 2026-10-10

## Perspective & Assigned Questions

P03 — simulated Anthropic staff software/AI engineer — examines execution, recovery and benchmark transfer. Incentive/blind spot: capability improvements and inspectable coding artifacts can make heterogeneous consumer transactions look easier than they are. P04 — simulated Microsoft enterprise privacy counsel/governance lead — examines continuing consent, authority and remedies. Incentive/blind spot: enterprise governance can overstate the practicality of consumer oversight or overlook its burden. These are distinct analytical lenses, not interviews, employee statements or expert testimony.

Frame: Products of Tomorrow; broad audience; personal and professional products; GenAI planning, tools, memory and delegated action. “Personal agents will become dominant over the app way of working” is the hypothesis under test. Family holiday remains an illustrative scenario. Read frame-initial.md, background.md, panel.md, expanded-map.md and questions-ex03.md. This bounded pass retains six new cards; no background 50-card quota applies. Core checkpoint was written before completion. All source access dates below: 2026-10-10.

## Question Answers

### Q016 — For a cross-app task that changes real state, can a personal agent detect partial completion, resume after interruption and prevent duplicate or contradictory actions with less total user work than a conventional app workflow?

**P03 answer:** Not established by this pass. Persisted progress, tests and recoverable code states are documented mechanisms (E-EX03-003/S-EX03-003, https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/effective-harnesses-for-long-running-agents). They strengthen the enabling account in E-BG-016/S-BG-015 without supplying an independently measured transaction recovery advantage. Current long-horizon evaluation identifies hidden-state and verification difficulties (E-EX03-001/S-EX03-001, https://arxiv.org/html/2606.29537v2). A working coding rollback cannot undo a supplier's nonrefundable charge.

Strongest counterevidence to a pessimistic answer: Anthropic reports that structured handoffs improve sustained progress; newer agents achieve substantial partial completion. This is evidence of engineering tractability, not proof of lower total household work. **Confidence:** high that the comparative claim remains unproved here; medium on transfer of mechanisms. **Limits:** no matched workflow comparison, injected interruption experiment, external transaction reconciliation or duplicate-action denominator retrieved. **Status:** partial. Broad links Q003,Q005,Q006,Q008.

### Q017 — Do gains on current computer-use benchmarks predict repeated safe completion and net time savings in messy real-world cross-app work, or do agents still depend on users performing enough verification to preserve app-led working?

**P03 answer:** Benchmark improvement is a capability signal; it does not establish the replacement proposition. E-EX03-001 shows a large gap between short-task benchmark scores and the authors' longer workflows, under specified model/harness/budget settings. E-EX03-002/S-EX03-002 (https://metr.org/blog/2026-02-24-uplift-update/) shows why a newer field estimate can also be hard to interpret. Current prospective benchmark-to-field calibration, repeated safe completion and net user-time savings remain unresolved.

Strongest counterevidence: the newer METR participants' willingness to keep using agentic tools and direction of raw estimates weaken a universal “AI slows work” claim. Do not carry early-2025 results forward as present frontier performance. Likewise, OSWorld 2.0 is a designed diagnostic evaluation, not a representative sample of actual consumer failures. **Confidence:** high that benchmark and field claims must be separated; low on net current cross-app savings. **Limits:** no same-system prospective field cohort matched to OSWorld 2.0, no representative oversight-time comparison. **Status:** partial. Broad Q006; links Q003,Q016. Historical background E-BG-009–012 and selected practitioners E-BG-036 retain their dates and scope.

### Q018 — Can a user meaningfully inspect, correct and revoke an agent's remembered preferences and connected-service access, including data already integrated into context or downstream systems, without having to abandon the agent entirely?

**P04 answer:** Selected products document useful granular controls; the end-to-end proposition remains unverified. Consumer Copilot's current version documents specific saved-memory deletion, which is meaningful counterevidence to treating Dots' granular-control limitation as universal (E-EX03-004/S-EX03-004, https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/privacy/microsoft-copilot/privacy-controls; existing Dots E-BG-031/S-BG-030 remains a separate product finding). Turning off saving and removing existing memories are different operations. Neither a memory switch nor a disconnected connector demonstrates erasure of past downstream copies. OpenAI's terms distinguish transfers to third-party applications (E-EX03-005), while EU GDPR distinguishes withdrawal, erasure and recipient notification (E-EX03-006).

Strongest counterevidence to confidence in controls: E-BG-031's already-integrated context remains after disconnection; E-BG-030 distinguishes multiple data uses. Strongest counterevidence to a blanket revocation failure claim: Copilot documents individual deletion and EU law supplies rights rather than leaving all deletion voluntary. **Confidence:** high on documented controls/legal text, low on independent efficacy and comprehension. **Limits:** no physical deletion test, downstream audit or household/enterprise usability study; consumer Microsoft account documentation does not cover Entra work/school accounts. **Status:** partial. Broad Q005,Q006; links Q003,Q019.

### Q019 — When a personal agent arranges and later changes a family holiday, whose authorization is required for shared personal data, nonrefundable spending and changes affecting other travelers, and who can correct or compensate an erroneous action?

**P04 answer:** There is no single verified household authorization and compensation rule in this evidence. Current OpenAI terms require permission for accounts/payment methods and allocate task oversight to the user (E-EX03-005/S-EX03-005, https://openai.com/policies/service-terms/). That provides a concrete contractual starting point, not a conclusion that the user bears every legal loss. Personal-data authority, payment authority and a supplier's booking/change authority must be traced separately; this separation is P04 inference, not a universal legal rule requiring every traveler to approve every action.

For GDPR-covered processing, the data subject's rights are relevant and privacy-infringement compensation is distinct from reimbursement for a bad booking (E-EX03-006/S-EX03-006, https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2016/679/oj/eng). A broad household instruction does not itself demonstrate permission to use another person's payment account. Existing provider handoff E-BG-033/S-BG-032 remains the supported journey boundary.

Strongest counterevidence to an agent-only responsibility story: contracts retain user oversight and payment-provider error routes; data rights and external supplier conditions survive delegation. Counterevidence to “no redress exists”: GDPR provides a complaint/remedy framework for its covered infringements. **Confidence:** high on selected terms and law, low on allocation for an actual family dispute. **Limits:** supplier contract, country, travel product, adult representation, minors' legal guardianship, payment mechanism and type of error unspecified; no decided agent-booking dispute or measured family workflow found. **Status:** partial. Broad Q005,Q008; links Q016,Q018.
